<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Every smooth connected projective complex variety with smoothly semipositive anticanonical bundle has a nonzero section of some positive anticanonical power.

- **claim-anticanonical-nonvanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-headline)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective variety over $\mathbb C$. If $-K_X$ admits a smooth Hermitian metric of semipositive curvature, then $H^0(X,-mK_X)\ne0$ for some integer $m>0$.
  - Explanation: If the anticanonical bundle of a smooth connected complex projective variety carries a smooth Hermitian metric of semipositive curvature, then some positive multiple of it has a nonzero section. The multiple is needed, since a first-power section can fail to exist.
- **claim-finite-volume-sections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-finite-volume)): Let $Z$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety of dimension $d$ with $H^0(Z,\Omega_Z^q)=0$ for every $1\le q\le d$. Let $D\ge0$ be an integral divisor. Suppose that $J=-K_Z+D$ has a semipositive singular Hermitian metric $h_J$ satisfying $$\int_Z \|s_D\|_{h_J}^2<\infty.$$ If a line bundle $L$ on $Z$ has a semipositive singular Hermitian metric with locally bounded weights, then there exist integers $a>0$ and $b\ge0$ such that $$H^0(Z,aL+bD)\ne0.$$
  - Explanation: On a variety with no positive-degree holomorphic forms, a finite adjoint volume metric on the anticanonical bundle twisted by an effective divisor, together with a bounded-weight semipositive metric on a line bundle, gives a section of a positive multiple of that line bundle with poles allowed only along the divisor. This is the inductive engine of the paper.
- **claim-finite-cover-torus-monodromy** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-structure)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety whose anticanonical bundle has a smooth Hermitian metric of semipositive Chern curvature. There are a connected finite étale cover $\nu:S\to X$, an integer $a\geq0$, and smooth connected projective varieties $F$ and $Z$ with the following properties.* 1. *For a suitable Kähler metric on $X$, the common universal cover of $X$ and $S$ has a holomorphic isometric decomposition $$\label{eq:cover-product} \widetilde X=\mathbb C^a\times F\times Z.$$ The compact factors are simply connected. The induced metric on $F$ is Ricci-flat, and $K_F$ has a nowhere-zero parallel holomorphic section. The anticanonical bundle of $Z$ is smoothly semipositive, and $$\label{eq:cover-noforms} H^0(Z,\Omega_Z^p)=0\quad(p>0),\qquad H^q(Z,\mathcal O_Z)=0\quad(q>0).$$ The variety $Z$ is rationally connected when it has positive dimension.* 2. *There are a full lattice $\Lambda\subset\mathbb C^a$ and a homomorphism $\rho:\Lambda\to\operatorname{Aut}(Z)$ for which $$\label{eq:cover-action} S=(\mathbb C^a\times F\times Z)/\Lambda,\qquad \lambda\cdot(z,f,v)=(z+\lambda,f,\rho(\lambda)v).$$ The closure $T$ of $\rho(\Lambda)$ in the holomorphic isometry group of $Z$ is a compact connected real torus. The trivial torus is allowed.* 3. *The action of $T$ extends to an algebraic action of a complex algebraic torus $G$ on $Z$, with $T$ Zariski dense in $G$. On $K_Z^{-1}=\det T_Z$ the action is the natural one induced by the differential. For every $m\geq0$, its representation on sections satisfies $$\label{eq:cover-invariants} H^0(Z,K_Z^{-m})^T=H^0(Z,K_Z^{-m})^G.$$* 4. *The canonical bundles of $\mathbb C^a$ and $F$ have nowhere-zero holomorphic frames $\eta_0$ and $\eta_F$ invariant under $\Lambda$. For every integer $m\geq0$, the product projections $\operatorname{pr}_0$, $\operatorname{pr}_F$, and $\operatorname{pr}_Z$ give the natural, $\Lambda$-equivariant identification $$\label{eq:cover-canonical} K_{\widetilde X}^{-m} =\operatorname{pr}_0^*K_{\mathbb C^a}^{-m} \otimes\operatorname{pr}_F^*K_F^{-m} \otimes\operatorname{pr}_Z^*K_Z^{-m},$$ in which the first two factors have the invariant frame $\eta_0^{-m}\otimes\eta_F^{-m}$.* *Any of the factors may be a point. A point contributes the canonical frame $1$; if $a=0$, the lattice is the zero group.
  - Explanation: A smoothly semipositive anticanonical metric yields a finite étale cover whose universal cover splits isometrically into a flat factor, a Ricci-flat factor and a compact factor without holomorphic forms. The residual deck action on the last factor lies in a compact torus that extends to an algebraic torus, and invariant canonical frames are retained.
- **lemma-etale-norm** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-etale-norm)): Let $\nu:S\to X$ be a finite étale morphism of degree $d>0$ between smooth connected projective complex varieties, and let $A$ be a line bundle on $X$. Every nonzero section $s\in H^0(S,\nu^*A)$ has a nonzero norm $$\operatorname{Nm}_{\nu}(s) \in H^0(X,A^{\otimes d}).$$ In particular, a nonzero section of $K_S^{-m}$, for $m>0$, gives a nonzero section of $K_X^{-md}$.
  - Explanation: The product of a section over the sheets of a finite étale cover defines a section of a tensor power of the line bundle on the base, and it is nonzero when the original section is nonzero. This returns a section from the cover to the original variety.
- **lemma-prepared-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-prepared-model)): Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective and $S_0$ an integral complex variety. Let $Z\dashrightarrow S_0$ be a dominant rational map with connected general fiber. There is a smooth projective birational model $S$ of $S_0$ and a diagram $$\begin{tikzcd}[row sep=large,column sep=large] Y\arrow[r,"r"]\arrow[dr,"f"']\arrow[rr,bend left=22,"\pi"] &\bar Y\arrow[r,"\bar\pi"]\arrow[d,"\bar f"]&Z\\ &S& \end{tikzcd}$$ in which $\bar Y$ is normal projective, $Y$ is smooth projective, $r$ and $\bar\pi$ are birational morphisms, and $\bar f$ is surjective and equidimensional. The construction may start above a prescribed birational model of $Z$. If an algebraic torus acts on $Z$ and the rational map is invariant, the diagram can be chosen equivariant with trivial action on $S$.* *Every prime divisor on $\bar Y$ either dominates $S$ or maps onto a prime divisor of $S$. At a general point of the strict transform in $Y$ of a prime $Q\subset\bar Y$ above a base prime $T$, there are coordinates in which $$\label{eq:power-map} f(t,x_2,\ldots,x_d)=(t^e,x_2,\ldots,x_s), \qquad e=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_Q(\bar f^*T)>0,$$ where $s=\dim S$ and $d=\dim Z$.
  - Explanation: A dominant rational map with connected general fibre can be replaced by a diagram with a normal model that is equidimensional over a smooth base and a smooth resolution above it, equivariantly if a torus acts. Vertical prime divisors then have local power-map coordinates.
- **lemma-slope-foliation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-slope-foliation)): Under the assumptions above, let $\mathcal F\subset T_Z$ be an algebraically integrable saturated foliation with $0<\operatorname{rank}\mathcal F<\dim Z$. Then the line bundle $$D-P=D+\det Q$$ admits a singular Hermitian metric of semipositive curvature. In particular, $c_1(Q)+[D]$ is pseudoeffective.
  - Explanation: For an algebraically integrable saturated foliation of intermediate rank, the relative Bergman metric with its ramification removed gives a semipositive singular metric on the divisor minus the determinant line of the quotient, so the corresponding class is pseudoeffective.
- **theorem-finite-volume-cotangent-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-finite-volume-slope)): Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective over $\mathbb C$, let $D\ge0$ be an integral divisor, and suppose that $J=-K_Z+D$ has a semipositive singular Hermitian metric $h_J$ with $\int_Z\|s_D\|_{h_J}^2<\infty$. If $\dim Z>0$, then every nonzero real movable class $\alpha$ satisfies $$\label{eq:h:slope:min} \mu_{\min,\alpha}(T_Z)\geq-D\cdot\alpha.$$ For every integer $k\geq0$ and every line bundle $M$ with a nonzero morphism $M\to(\Omega_Z^1)^{\otimes k}$, the line bundle $$\label{eq:h:slope:line} -M+kD$$ is pseudoeffective. More generally, if $E$ is a torsion-free subsheaf of rank $r>0$ in $(\Omega_Z^1)^{\otimes k}$, then $-\det E+krD$ is pseudoeffective.
  - Explanation: Finite adjoint volume bounds the minimal slope of the tangent sheaf from below against every nonzero movable class by minus the pairing with the divisor, and consequently cotangent tensors and their determinants are controlled by pseudoeffective classes.
- **corollary-supporting-class** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-slope-face)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:finite-volume-slope" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:finite-volume-slope">7</a>, assume $\dim Z>0$ and let $L$ be a pseudoeffective line bundle for which $L+D$ is not big. There exists a nonzero real movable class $\alpha$ with $$L\cdot\alpha=D\cdot\alpha=0.$$ For this class $\mu_{\min,\alpha}(T_Z)\geq0$. Suppose moreover that $M\to(\Omega_Z^1)^{\otimes k}$ is nonzero and that effective divisors $N_i\sim M+m_iL$ are given. Then $$M\cdot\alpha=N_i\cdot\alpha=0 \quad\text{for every }i.$$ Every finite nonnegative linear combination of $D,N_1,N_2,\ldots$ has zero intersection with $\alpha$ and is not big.
  - Explanation: If L is pseudoeffective and L plus D is not big, a supporting movable class annihilates both L and D, and then also the determinant lines and effective divisors built from them. Hence no nonnegative combination of them is big.
- **proposition-two-metric-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-transfer)): Let $X,S,Y$ be smooth connected projective varieties over $\mathbb C$, and let $\bar Y$ be a normal projective variety. Suppose $r:Y\to\bar Y$ and $\bar\pi:\bar Y\to X$ are projective birational morphisms, and $\bar f:\bar Y\to S$ is a surjective equidimensional morphism with connected general fiber. Put $\pi=\bar\pi r$ and $f=\bar f r$, as in Figure <a href="#fig:models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:models">1</a>.* *Let $D$ be an effective integral divisor on $X$, and let $J=-K_X+D$ have a semipositive singular metric $h_J$ with $$\int_X \|s_D\|_{h_J}^2<\infty.$$ Let $L$ be a line bundle on $X$ with a semipositive metric $h_L$ of locally bounded weights. Suppose that for an integer $k>0$ there is a nonzero rational section $\rho$ of $k\bar\pi^*L$ with no horizontal poles, and that $$\label{eq:rank} \dim_{\mathbb C(S)} H^0\!\left(Y_\eta, \left(K_{Y/S}+\pi^*(J+jkL)\right)\|_{Y_\eta}\right)=1 \qquad(j=0,1,2,\ldots).$$ Here $\eta$ is the generic point of $S$ and $Y_\eta=Y\times_S\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb C(S)$.* *After replacing $k,\rho$ by $dk,\rho^d$ for some integer $d>0$, there are a line bundle $A$ on $S$, an effective integral divisor $D_S$, and a nonzero regular section $$e\in H^0(\bar Y,k\bar\pi^*L-\bar f^*A)$$ with these properties:* 1. *$A$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights.* 2. *$-K_S+D_S$ has a semipositive singular metric $h_S$ with $\int_S\|s_{D_S}\|_{h_S}^2<\infty$.* 3. *$D_S$ is supported on base primes $T$ for which every prime component of $\bar f^*T$ is $\bar\pi$-exceptional or lies in $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}(\bar\pi^*D)$.* 4. *If $a>0,b\ge0$ are integers and $H^0(S,aA+bD_S)\ne0$, then $H^0(X,akL+cD)\ne0$ for some integer $c\ge0$.* *In these conclusions $k$ denotes the enlarged integer.
  - Explanation: Given a prepared diagram and an adjoint rank-one condition in every degree, one obtains on the lower-dimensional base a line bundle with a bounded-weight semipositive metric and an effective divisor whose anticanonical twist has a finite-volume semipositive metric, such that sections on the base lift to sections with permitted poles upstairs.
- **lemma-fixed-determinant** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-determinant)): Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective of dimension $d>0$, with $H^0(Z,\Omega_Z^q)=0$ for $1\le q\le d$. Let $L$ have a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights, and let $\alpha$ be a nonzero movable class satisfying $\mu_{\min,\alpha}(T_Z)\ge0$. There are a line bundle $M$ with $M\cdot\alpha\le0$, strictly increasing positive integers $m_i\to\infty$, and nonzero sections $$s_i\in H^0(Z,M+m_iL).$$ The line $M$ is the determinant of a saturated subsheaf of $\Omega_Z^p$ for some $0\le p\le d$.
  - Explanation: Riemann–Roch, Hodge symmetry and hard Lefschetz with trivial multiplier ideals produce a fixed determinant line of non-positive movable degree and sections of its twists by arbitrarily high multiples of L.
- **lemma-maximal-base** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-maximal-base)): Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective of positive dimension $d$. Let $L,M$ be line bundles and $D\ge0$ an integral divisor. Suppose there is a nonzero movable class $\alpha$ with $$L\cdot\alpha=D\cdot\alpha=0,\qquad M\cdot\alpha\le0,$$ and nonzero sections $s_i\in H^0(Z,M+m_iL)$ for strictly increasing positive integers $m_i\to\infty$. There is a smooth projective base $S$ with $\dim S<d$, together with the prepared diagram of Lemma <a href="#lem:prepared-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:prepared-model">5</a> and an integer $k>0$, for which:* 1. *a nonzero rational section $\rho$ of $k\bar\pi^*L$ has no horizontal poles on $\bar Y$;* 2. *putting $J=-K_Z+D$, one has for every integer $j\ge0$ $$\label{eq:induction-ranks} \dim_{\mathbb C(S)}H^0\!\left(Y_\eta, \left(K_{Y/S}+\pi^*(J+jkL)\right)\|_{Y_\eta}\right)=1.$$* *If $S$ is a point, assertion <span class="upright">(i)</span> already gives a nonzero section of $kL$ on $Z$.
  - Explanation: A linear system of maximal image dimension among the allowed systems gives a strictly lower-dimensional base. Ratios of sections lie in its function field, yielding a rational section without horizontal poles and adjoint rank one in every degree.
- **proposition-invariant-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-invariant-transfer)): Use the varieties, maps, line bundles, and section of Proposition <a href="#prop:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:transfer">9</a>, with $D=0$. Let a compact torus $T$ act holomorphically and continuously on $X,\bar Y,Y$, with compatible holomorphic linearizations of the line bundles. Require $r,\bar\pi,\bar f$ to be equivariant, with trivial action on $S$. Require $\rho$ and the $J$-valued adjoint section $\eta_Y=\pi^*s_0$ to be invariant, and require $h_J,h_L$ to be smooth, semipositive, and $T$-invariant.* *Replace the ordinary rank hypothesis <a href="#eq:rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rank">[eq:rank]</a> by $$\dim_{\mathbb C(S)} H^0\!\left(Y_\eta, \left(K_{Y/S}+\pi^*(J+jkL)\right)\|_{Y_\eta}\right)^T=1 \qquad(j=0,1,2,\ldots).$$ Then all the conclusions of Proposition <a href="#prop:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:transfer">9</a> hold, and the section on $X$ produced by its lifting is $T$-invariant.
  - Explanation: With smooth invariant metrics and a compact torus action, rank one of the invariant adjoint space suffices. Berndtsson positivity plus orthogonal averaging gives the transfer conclusions, and the lifted section is invariant.
- **theorem-invariant-nonvanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-invariant)): Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective over $\mathbb C$, with $H^0(Z,\Omega_Z^q)=0$ for every $q>0$. Suppose that $L=-K_Z$ has a smooth Hermitian metric of semipositive curvature. Let a compact connected real torus $T$ act continuously on $Z$ by holomorphic automorphisms, and give $L$ the natural linearization induced by the differential of the action. Then $$H^0(Z,mL)^T\ne0 \quad\text{for some integer }m>0.$$
  - Explanation: On a variety with no holomorphic forms, a smoothly semipositive anticanonical bundle and a compact connected torus action with natural linearization give a nonzero invariant section of some positive anticanonical power.

The induced metric on $F$ is Ricci-flat, and $K_F$ has a nowhere-zero parallel holomorphic section. The anticanonical bundle of $Z$ is smoothly semipositive, and $$\\label{eq:cover-noforms}\n     H^0(Z,\\Omega_Z^p)=0\\quad(p>0),\\qquad\n     H^q(Z,\\mathcal O_Z)=0\\quad(q>0).$$ The variety $Z$ is rationally connected when it has positive dimension.*\n\n\n2.  *There are a full lattice $\\Lambda\\subset\\mathbb C^a$ and a homomorphism $\\rho:\\Lambda\\to\\operatorname{Aut}(Z)$ for which $$\\label{eq:cover-action}\n     S=(\\mathbb C^a\\times F\\times Z)/\\Lambda,\\qquad\n     \\lambda\\cdot(z,f,v)=(z+\\lambda,f,\\rho(\\lambda)v).$$ The closure $T$ of $\\rho(\\Lambda)$ in the holomorphic isometry group of $Z$ is a compact connected real torus. The trivial torus is allowed.*\n\n\n3.  *The action of $T$ extends to an algebraic action of a complex algebraic torus $G$ on $Z$, with $T$ Zariski dense in $G$. On $K_Z^{-1}=\\det T_Z$ the action is the natural one induced by the differential. For every $m\\geq0$, its representation on sections satisfies $$\\label{eq:cover-invariants}\n           H^0(Z,K_Z^{-m})^T=H^0(Z,K_Z^{-m})^G.$$*\n\n\n4.  *The canonical bundles of $\\mathbb C^a$ and $F$ have nowhere-zero holomorphic frames $\\eta_0$ and $\\eta_F$ invariant under $\\Lambda$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-etale-norm",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\nu:S\\to X$ be a finite étale morphism of degree $d>0$ between smooth connected projective complex varieties, and let $A$ be a line bundle on $X$. Every nonzero section $s\\in H^0(S,\\nu^*A)$ has a nonzero norm $$\\operatorname{Nm}_{\\nu}(s)\n                    \\in H^0(X,A^{\\otimes d}).$$ In particular, a nonzero section of $K_S^{-m}$, for $m>0$, gives a nonzero section of $K_X^{-md}$.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective and $S_0$ an integral complex variety. Let $Z\\dashrightarrow S_0$ be a dominant rational map with connected general fiber. If an algebraic torus acts on $Z$ and the rational map is invariant, the diagram can be chosen equivariant with trivial action on $S$.*\n\n\n*Every prime divisor on $\\bar Y$ either dominates $S$ or maps onto a prime divisor of $S$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-finite-volume-slope",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"h:slope:cotangent\" label=\"h:slope:cotangent\"></span>Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective over $\\mathbb C$, let $D\\ge0$ be an integral divisor, and suppose that $J=-K_Z+D$ has a semipositive singular Hermitian metric $h_J$ with $\\int_Z|s_D|_{h_J}^2<\\infty$. If $\\dim Z>0$, then every nonzero real movable class $\\alpha$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:h:slope:min}\n                  \\mu_{\\min,\\alpha}(T_Z)\\geq-D\\cdot\\alpha.$$ For every integer $k\\geq0$ and every line bundle $M$ with a nonzero morphism $M\\to(\\Omega_Z^1)^{\\otimes k}$, the line bundle $$\\label{eq:h:slope:line}\n                             -M+kD$$ is pseudoeffective. More generally, if $E$ is a torsion-free subsheaf of rank $r>0$ in $(\\Omega_Z^1)^{\\otimes k}$, then $-\\det E+krD$ is pseudoeffective.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-finite-volume-slope",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"h:slope:cotangent\" label=\"h:slope:cotangent\"></span>Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective over $\\mathbb C$, let $D\\ge0$ be an integral divisor, and suppose that $J=-K_Z+D$ has a semipositive singular Hermitian metric $h_J$ with $\\int_Z|s_D|_{h_J}^2<\\infty$. If $\\dim Z>0$, then every nonzero real movable class $\\alpha$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:h:slope:min}\n                  \\mu_{\\min,\\alpha}(T_Z)\\geq-D\\cdot\\alpha.$$ For every integer $k\\geq0$ and every line bundle $M$ with a nonzero morphism $M\\to(\\Omega_Z^1)^{\\otimes k}$, the line bundle $$\\label{eq:h:slope:line}\n                             -M+kD$$ is pseudoeffective. More generally, if $E$ is a torsion-free subsheaf of rank $r>0$ in $(\\Omega_Z^1)^{\\otimes k}$, then $-\\det E+krD$ is pseudoeffective.",
          "source_id": "PM-068.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"h:slope:cotangent\" label=\"h:slope:cotangent\"></span>Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective over $\\mathbb C$, let $D\\ge0$ be an integral divisor, and suppose that $J=-K_Z+D$ has a semipositive singular Hermitian metric $h_J$ with $\\int_Z|s_D|_{h_J}^2<\\infty$. If $\\dim Z>0$, then every nonzero real movable class $\\alpha$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:h:slope:min}\n                  \\mu_{\\min,\\alpha}(T_Z)\\geq-D\\cdot\\alpha.$$ For every integer $k\\geq0$ and every line bundle $M$ with a nonzero morphism $M\\to(\\Omega_Z^1)^{\\otimes k}$, the line bundle $$\\label{eq:h:slope:line}\n                             -M+kD$$ is pseudoeffective. More generally, if $E$ is a torsion-free subsheaf of rank $r>0$ in $(\\Omega_Z^1)^{\\otimes k}$, then $-\\det E+krD$ is pseudoeffective.",
      "explanation": "Finite adjoint volume bounds the minimal slope of the tangent sheaf from below against every nonzero movable class by minus the pairing with the divisor, and consequently cotangent tensors and their determinants are controlled by pseudoeffective classes.",

      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-slope-face",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:finite-volume-slope\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:finite-volume-slope\">7</a>, assume $\\dim Z>0$ and let $L$ be a pseudoeffective line bundle for which $L+D$ is not big. There exists a nonzero real movable class $\\alpha$ with $$L\\cdot\\alpha=D\\cdot\\alpha=0.$$ For this class $\\mu_{\\min,\\alpha}(T_Z)\\geq0$. Suppose moreover that $M\\to(\\Omega_Z^1)^{\\otimes k}$ is nonzero and that effective divisors $N_i\\sim M+m_iL$ are given. Then $$M\\cdot\\alpha=N_i\\cdot\\alpha=0\n                         \\quad\\text{for every }i.$$ Every finite nonnegative linear combination of $D,N_1,N_2,\\ldots$ has zero intersection with $\\alpha$ and is not big.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "exact_statement": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:finite-volume-slope\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:finite-volume-slope\">7</a>, assume $\\dim Z>0$ and let $L$ be a pseudoeffective line bundle for which $L+D$ is not big.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X,S,Y$ be smooth connected projective varieties over $\\mathbb C$, and let $\\bar Y$ be a normal projective variety. Suppose $r:Y\\to\\bar Y$ and $\\bar\\pi:\\bar Y\\to X$ are projective birational morphisms, and $\\bar f:\\bar Y\\to S$ is a surjective equidimensional morphism with connected general fiber. Put $\\pi=\\bar\\pi r$ and $f=\\bar f r$, as in Figure <a href=\"#fig:models\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"fig:models\">1</a>.*\n\n\n*Let $D$ be an effective integral divisor on $X$, and let $J=-K_X+D$ have a semipositive singular metric $h_J$ with $$\\int_X |s_D|_{h_J}^2<\\infty.$$ Let $L$ be a line bundle on $X$ with a semipositive metric $h_L$ of locally bounded weights. Suppose that for an integer $k>0$ there is a nonzero rational section $\\rho$ of $k\\bar\\pi^*L$ with no horizontal poles, and that $$\\label{eq:rank}\n \\dim_{\\mathbb C(S)}\n H^0\\!\\left(Y_\\eta,\n  \\left(K_{Y/S}+\\pi^*(J+jkL)\\right)|_{Y_\\eta}\\right)=1\n \\qquad(j=0,1,2,\\ldots).$$ Here $\\eta$ is the generic point of $S$ and $Y_\\eta=Y\\times_S\\operatorname{Spec}\\mathbb C(S)$.*\n\n*After replacing $k,\\rho$ by $dk,\\rho^d$ for some integer $d>0$, there are a line bundle $A$ on $S$, an effective integral divisor $D_S$, and a nonzero regular section $$e\\in H^0(\\bar Y,k\\bar\\pi^*L-\\bar f^*A)$$ with these properties:*\n\n1.  *$A$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights.*\n\n2.  *$-K_S+D_S$ has a semipositive singular metric $h_S$ with $\\int_S|s_{D_S}|_{h_S}^2<\\infty$.*\n\n3.  *$D_S$ is supported on base primes $T$ for which every prime component of $\\bar f^*T$ is $\\bar\\pi$-exceptional or lies in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}(\\bar\\pi^*D)$.*\n\n4.  *If $a>0,b\\ge0$ are integers and $H^0(S,aA+bD_S)\\ne0$, then $H^0(X,akL+cD)\\ne0$ for some integer $c\\ge0$.*\n\n*In these conclusions $k$ denotes the enlarged integer.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X,S,Y$ be smooth connected projective varieties over $\\mathbb C$, and let $\\bar Y$ be a normal projective variety. Suppose $r:Y\\to\\bar Y$ and $\\bar\\pi:\\bar Y\\to X$ are projective birational morphisms, and $\\bar f:\\bar Y\\to S$ is a surjective equidimensional morphism with connected general fiber. Put $\\pi=\\bar\\pi r$ and $f=\\bar f r$, as in Figure <a href=\"#fig:models\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"fig:models\">1</a>.*\n\n\n*Let $D$ be an effective integral divisor on $X$, and let $J=-K_X+D$ have a semipositive singular metric $h_J$ with $$\\int_X |s_D|_{h_J}^2<\\infty.$$ Let $L$ be a line bundle on $X$ with a semipositive metric $h_L$ of locally bounded weights. Suppose that for an integer $k>0$ there is a nonzero rational section $\\rho$ of $k\\bar\\pi^*L$ with no horizontal poles, and that $$\\label{eq:rank}\n \\dim_{\\mathbb C(S)}\n H^0\\!\\left(Y_\\eta,\n  \\left(K_{Y/S}+\\pi^*(J+jkL)\\right)|_{Y_\\eta}\\right)=1\n \\qquad(j=0,1,2,\\ldots).$$ Here $\\eta$ is the generic point of $S$ and $Y_\\eta=Y\\times_S\\operatorname{Spec}\\mathbb C(S)$.*\n\n*After replacing $k,\\rho$ by $dk,\\rho^d$ for some integer $d>0$, there are a line bundle $A$ on $S$, an effective integral divisor $D_S$, and a nonzero regular section $$e\\in H^0(\\bar Y,k\\bar\\pi^*L-\\bar f^*A)$$ with these properties:*\n\n1.  *$A$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights.*\n\n2.  *$-K_S+D_S$ has a semipositive singular metric $h_S$ with $\\int_S|s_{D_S}|_{h_S}^2<\\infty$.*\n\n3.  *$D_S$ is supported on base primes $T$ for which every prime component of $\\bar f^*T$ is $\\bar\\pi$-exceptional or lies in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}(\\bar\\pi^*D)$.*\n\n4.  *If $a>0,b\\ge0$ are integers and $H^0(S,aA+bD_S)\\ne0$, then $H^0(X,akL+cD)\\ne0$ for some integer $c\\ge0$.*\n\n*In these conclusions $k$ denotes the enlarged integer.",
          "source_id": "PM-068.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $X,S,Y$ be smooth connected projective varieties over $\\mathbb C$, and let $\\bar Y$ be a normal projective variety. Suppose $r:Y\\to\\bar Y$ and $\\bar\\pi:\\bar Y\\to X$ are projective birational morphisms, and $\\bar f:\\bar Y\\to S$ is a surjective equidimensional morphism with connected general fiber. Put $\\pi=\\bar\\pi r$ and $f=\\bar f r$, as in Figure <a href=\"#fig:models\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"fig:models\">1</a>.*\n\n\n*Let $D$ be an effective integral divisor on $X$, and let $J=-K_X+D$ have a semipositive singular metric $h_J$ with $$\\int_X |s_D|_{h_J}^2<\\infty.$$ Let $L$ be a line bundle on $X$ with a semipositive metric $h_L$ of locally bounded weights. Suppose that for an integer $k>0$ there is a nonzero rational section $\\rho$ of $k\\bar\\pi^*L$ with no horizontal poles, and that $$\\label{eq:rank}\n \\dim_{\\mathbb C(S)}\n H^0\\!\\left(Y_\\eta,\n  \\left(K_{Y/S}+\\pi^*(J+jkL)\\right)|_{Y_\\eta}\\right)=1\n \\qquad(j=0,1,2,\\ldots).$$ Here $\\eta$ is the generic point of $S$ and $Y_\\eta=Y\\times_S\\operatorname{Spec}\\mathbb C(S)$.*\n\n*After replacing $k,\\rho$ by $dk,\\rho^d$ for some integer $d>0$, there are a line bundle $A$ on $S$, an effective integral divisor $D_S$, and a nonzero regular section $$e\\in H^0(\\bar Y,k\\bar\\pi^*L-\\bar f^*A)$$ with these properties:*\n\n1.  *$A$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights.*\n\n2.  *$-K_S+D_S$ has a semipositive singular metric $h_S$ with $\\int_S|s_{D_S}|_{h_S}^2<\\infty$.*\n\n3.  *$D_S$ is supported on base primes $T$ for which every prime component of $\\bar f^*T$ is $\\bar\\pi$-exceptional or lies in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}(\\bar\\pi^*D)$.*\n\n4.  *If $a>0,b\\ge0$ are integers and $H^0(S,aA+bD_S)\\ne0$, then $H^0(X,akL+cD)\\ne0$ for some integer $c\\ge0$.*\n\n*In these conclusions $k$ denotes the enlarged integer.",
      "explanation": "Given a prepared diagram and an adjoint rank-one condition in every degree, one obtains on the lower-dimensional base a line bundle with a bounded-weight semipositive metric and an effective divisor whose anticanonical twist has a finite-volume semipositive metric, such that sections on the base lift to sections with permitted poles upstairs.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-determinant",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective of dimension $d>0$, with $H^0(Z,\\Omega_Z^q)=0$ for $1\\le q\\le d$. Let $L$ have a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights, and let $\\alpha$ be a nonzero movable class satisfying $\\mu_{\\min,\\alpha}(T_Z)\\ge0$. There are a line bundle $M$ with $M\\cdot\\alpha\\le0$, strictly increasing positive integers $m_i\\to\\infty$, and nonzero sections $$s_i\\in H^0(Z,M+m_iL).$$ The line $M$ is the determinant of a saturated subsheaf of $\\Omega_Z^p$ for some $0\\le p\\le d$.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-determinant",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective of dimension $d>0$, with $H^0(Z,\\Omega_Z^q)=0$ for $1\\le q\\le d$. There are a line bundle $M$ with $M\\cdot\\alpha\\le0$, strictly increasing positive integers $m_i\\to\\infty$, and nonzero sections $$s_i\\in H^0(Z,M+m_iL).$$ The line $M$ is the determinant of a saturated subsheaf of $\\Omega_Z^p$ for some $0\\le p\\le d$.",
          "source_id": "PM-068.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective of dimension $d>0$, with $H^0(Z,\\Omega_Z^q)=0$ for $1\\le q\\le d$. There are a line bundle $M$ with $M\\cdot\\alpha\\le0$, strictly increasing positive integers $m_i\\to\\infty$, and nonzero sections $$s_i\\in H^0(Z,M+m_iL).$$ The line $M$ is the determinant of a saturated subsheaf of $\\Omega_Z^p$ for some $0\\le p\\le d$.",
      "explanation": "Riemann–Roch, Hodge symmetry and hard Lefschetz with trivial multiplier ideals produce a fixed determinant line of non-positive movable degree and sections of its twists by arbitrarily high multiples of L.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-maximal-base",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective of positive dimension $d$. Let $L,M$ be line bundles and $D\\ge0$ an integral divisor. Suppose there is a nonzero movable class $\\alpha$ with $$L\\cdot\\alpha=D\\cdot\\alpha=0,\\qquad M\\cdot\\alpha\\le0,$$ and nonzero sections $s_i\\in H^0(Z,M+m_iL)$ for strictly increasing positive integers $m_i\\to\\infty$. There is a smooth projective base $S$ with $\\dim S<d$, together with the prepared diagram of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:prepared-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:prepared-model\">5</a> and an integer $k>0$, for which:*\n\n1.  *a nonzero rational section $\\rho$ of $k\\bar\\pi^*L$ has no horizontal poles on $\\bar Y$;*\n\n\n2.  *putting $J=-K_Z+D$, one has for every integer $j\\ge0$ $$\\label{eq:induction-ranks}\n     \\dim_{\\mathbb C(S)}H^0\\!\\left(Y_\\eta,\n       \\left(K_{Y/S}+\\pi^*(J+jkL)\\right)|_{Y_\\eta}\\right)=1.$$*\n\n*If $S$ is a point, assertion <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> already gives a nonzero section of $kL$ on $Z$.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-maximal-base",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective of positive dimension $d$. Let $L,M$ be line bundles and $D\\ge0$ an integral divisor. There is a smooth projective base $S$ with $\\dim S<d$, together with the prepared diagram of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:prepared-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:prepared-model\">5</a> and an integer $k>0$, for which:*\n\n1.  *a nonzero rational section $\\rho$ of $k\\bar\\pi^*L$ has no horizontal poles on $\\bar Y$;*\n\n\n2.  *putting $J=-K_Z+D$, one has for every integer $j\\ge0$ $$\\label{eq:induction-ranks}\n     \\dim_{\\mathbb C(S)}H^0\\!\\left(Y_\\eta,\n       \\left(K_{Y/S}+\\pi^*(J+jkL)\\right)|_{Y_\\eta}\\right)=1.$$*\n\n*If $S$ is a point, assertion <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> already gives a nonzero section of $kL$ on $Z$.",
          "source_id": "PM-068.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective of positive dimension $d$. Let $L,M$ be line bundles and $D\\ge0$ an integral divisor. There is a smooth projective base $S$ with $\\dim S<d$, together with the prepared diagram of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:prepared-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:prepared-model\">5</a> and an integer $k>0$, for which:*\n\n1.  *a nonzero rational section $\\rho$ of $k\\bar\\pi^*L$ has no horizontal poles on $\\bar Y$;*\n\n\n2.  *putting $J=-K_Z+D$, one has for every integer $j\\ge0$ $$\\label{eq:induction-ranks}\n     \\dim_{\\mathbb C(S)}H^0\\!\\left(Y_\\eta,\n       \\left(K_{Y/S}+\\pi^*(J+jkL)\\right)|_{Y_\\eta}\\right)=1.$$*\n\n*If $S$ is a point, assertion <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> already gives a nonzero section of $kL$ on $Z$.",
      "explanation": "A linear system of maximal image dimension among the allowed systems gives a strictly lower-dimensional base. Ratios of sections lie in its function field, yielding a rational section without horizontal poles and adjoint rank one in every degree.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-invariant-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Use the varieties, maps, line bundles, and section of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:transfer\">9</a>, with $D=0$. Let a compact torus $T$ act holomorphically and continuously on $X,\\bar Y,Y$, with compatible holomorphic linearizations of the line bundles. Require $r,\\bar\\pi,\\bar f$ to be equivariant, with trivial action on $S$. Require $\\rho$ and the $J$-valued adjoint section $\\eta_Y=\\pi^*s_0$ to be invariant, and require $h_J,h_L$ to be smooth, semipositive, and $T$-invariant.*\n\n*Replace the ordinary rank hypothesis <a href=\"#eq:rank\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rank\">[eq:rank]</a> by $$\\dim_{\\mathbb C(S)}\n H^0\\!\\left(Y_\\eta,\n \\left(K_{Y/S}+\\pi^*(J+jkL)\\right)|_{Y_\\eta}\\right)^T=1\n \\qquad(j=0,1,2,\\ldots).$$ Then all the conclusions of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:transfer\">9</a> hold, and the section on $X$ produced by its lifting is $T$-invariant.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-invariant-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Use the varieties, maps, line bundles, and section of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:transfer\">9</a>, with $D=0$. Let a compact torus $T$ act holomorphically and continuously on $X,\\bar Y,Y$, with compatible holomorphic linearizations of the line bundles. Require $r,\\bar\\pi,\\bar f$ to be equivariant, with trivial action on $S$. Require $\\rho$ and the $J$-valued adjoint section $\\eta_Y=\\pi^*s_0$ to be invariant, and require $h_J,h_L$ to be smooth, semipositive, and $T$-invariant.*\n\n*Replace the ordinary rank hypothesis <a href=\"#eq:rank\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rank\">[eq:rank]</a> by $$\\dim_{\\mathbb C(S)}\n H^0\\!\\left(Y_\\eta,\n \\left(K_{Y/S}+\\pi^*(J+jkL)\\right)|_{Y_\\eta}\\right)^T=1\n \\qquad(j=0,1,2,\\ldots).$$ Then all the conclusions of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:transfer\">9</a> hold, and the section on $X$ produced by its lifting is $T$-invariant.",
          "source_id": "PM-068.1"

      "exact_statement": "Use the varieties, maps, line bundles, and section of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:transfer\">9</a>, with $D=0$. Let a compact torus $T$ act holomorphically and continuously on $X,\\bar Y,Y$, with compatible holomorphic linearizations of the line bundles. Require $r,\\bar\\pi,\\bar f$ to be equivariant, with trivial action on $S$. Require $\\rho$ and the $J$-valued adjoint section $\\eta_Y=\\pi^*s_0$ to be invariant, and require $h_J,h_L$ to be smooth, semipositive, and $T$-invariant.*\n\n*Replace the ordinary rank hypothesis <a href=\"#eq:rank\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rank\">[eq:rank]</a> by $$\\dim_{\\mathbb C(S)}\n H^0\\!\\left(Y_\\eta,\n \\left(K_{Y/S}+\\pi^*(J+jkL)\\right)|_{Y_\\eta}\\right)^T=1\n \\qquad(j=0,1,2,\\ldots).$$ Then all the conclusions of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:transfer\">9</a> hold, and the section on $X$ produced by its lifting is $T$-invariant.",
      "explanation": "With smooth invariant metrics and a compact torus action, rank one of the invariant adjoint space suffices. Berndtsson positivity plus orthogonal averaging gives the transfer conclusions, and the lifted section is invariant.",

Then $$H^0(Z,mL)^T\\ne0\n                  \\quad\\text{for some integer }m>0.$$",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **anticanonical line bundle** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a smooth projective complex variety $X$, the anticanonical line bundle is $-K_X=\det T_X$.
- **smoothly semipositive metric** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A smooth Hermitian metric on this line is semipositive when its Chern curvature is a nonnegative real $(1,1)$-form.
- **additive notation for line bundles** ([source](#sec:overview)): We use additive notation: $J+L$ means $J\otimes L$, and $mL$ means $L^{\otimes m}$.
- **canonical section s_D** ([source](#sec:overview)): For an effective integral divisor $D$, write $s_D$ for the canonical section of $\mathcal O_Z(D)$.
- **singular semipositive metric with bounded weights** ([source](#sec:overview)): A singular metric has local squared frame norm $e^{-\phi}$. It is semipositive when $\phi$ is plurisubharmonic; locally bounded weights means bounds on both sides.
- **saturated foliation** ([source](#pm-h-11)): A saturated foliation $\mathcal F\subset T_Z$ is a coherent subsheaf with torsion-free quotient, closed under the Lie bracket.
- **movable class** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A movable class is an element of the closed movable cone $\operatorname{Mov}_1(Z)\subset N_1(Z)_{\mathbb R}$.
- **horizontal and vertical primes** ([source](#sec:transfer)): A prime is called *horizontal* if it dominates the base, and *vertical* otherwise.
- **relative canonical divisor K_{Y/S}** ([source](#sec:transfer)): For a morphism $f:Y\to S$ of smooth varieties we write $K_{Y/S}=K_Y-f^*K_S$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-etale-norm** [claim](#stmt-lem-etale-norm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-etale-norm): Local product of the sheetwise coefficients, gluing by frame change, nonvanishing off the image of the zero set. -> nonzero section of a tensor power downstairs
- **step-structure** [claim](#stmt-thm-structure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-structure): Prescribed-Ricci metric, de Rham splitting, Bochner vanishing, Bieberbach lattice, algebraic torus closure. -> finite cover with compact torus monodromy
- **step-prepared-model** [claim](#stmt-lem-prepared-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prepared-model): Resolve the graph, flatten, take the main component, normalize, resolve. -> normal equidimensional model and smooth resolution
- **step-slope-foliation** [claim](#stmt-h-slope-foliation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-slope-foliation): Relative Bergman metric corrected by the ramification order, psh extension across divisors. -> semipositive metric on the corrected line
- **step-slope-bound** [claim](#stmt-thm-finite-volume-slope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-finite-volume-slope): Harder–Narasimhan filtration, bracket vanishing, algebraicity criterion, contradiction. -> lower slope bound and pseudoeffective twisted determinants
- **step-supporting-class** [claim](#stmt-h-slope-face) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-slope-face): Supporting hyperplane and cone duality. -> movable class annihilating L and D
- **step-determinant** [claim](#stmt-lem-determinant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-determinant): Riemann–Roch, hard Lefschetz with multiplier ideals, stabilization of generic subspaces, exterior product. -> fixed determinant line with unbounded twisted sections
- **step-maximal-base** [claim](#stmt-lem-maximal-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-maximal-base): Maximal image dimension, relative algebraic closure, interpolation of sections, clearing vertical poles. -> smaller base, section without horizontal poles, adjoint rank one
- **step-transfer** [claim](#stmt-prop-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-transfer): Divisorial normalization, fibre integral moments, Bergman positivity, envelope of normalized moments, lifting. -> two base metrics and lifting of sections
- **step-finite-volume-induction** [claim](#stmt-thm-finite-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-maximal-base-2): Induction on dimension, by contradiction. -> section of a positive multiple of L with poles on D
- **step-invariant-transfer** [claim](#stmt-prop-invariant-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-invariant-transfer): Berndtsson positivity and orthogonal averaging over the compact torus. -> transfer with invariant lifted section
- **step-invariant-nonvanishing** [claim](#stmt-thm-invariant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-invariant): Algebraic torus, Rosenlicht quotient, fundamental vector fields, invariant transfer, induction theorem on the base. -> nonzero invariant section of a positive power
- **step-headline** [claim](#stmt-thm-headline) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-invariant-2): Product of invariant frames and the invariant section, descent to the cover, finite étale norm. -> nonzero anticanonical section on the original variety

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-anticanonical-nonvanishing**: For a smooth connected complex projective variety whose anticanonical bundle has a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric, some positive multiple of the anticanonical bundle has a nonzero section. The exponent is existential and not controlled.
- **inv-finite-volume-sections**: On a smooth connected projective variety with no positive-degree holomorphic forms, an integrable adjoint volume metric together with a bounded-weight semipositive metric on L yields a nonzero section of aL+bD for some integers a>0 and b>=0.
- **inv-etale-norm**: For a finite étale cover of positive degree, a nonzero section of the pulled-back line bundle has a nonzero norm section of the tensor power of that degree on the base.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-first-power-section**: Expect a nonzero section of the anticanonical bundle itself, rather than of some positive multiple. -> The result only gives some positive multiple; the first power can have no section even when the metric is smoothly semipositive.
- **anti-infinite-adjoint-volume**: Apply the finite-volume nonvanishing theorem when the adjoint measure has infinite total mass. -> The induction needs integrability of the adjoint measure to build the base metrics; without it no conclusion follows.
- **anti-numerical-effectivity**: Conclude existence of a section from numerical effectivity or nefness alone. -> Numerical equivalence to an effective divisor does not give a section; linear effectivity is needed.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existence theorems in complex algebraic and analytic geometry, proved by contradiction and induction on dimension using resolutions, relative Bergman metrics, plurisubharmonic envelopes and cohomology vanishing. There is no finite input-to-output procedure, and the exponents and sections are obtained non-effectively, so no executable reference implementation with an independent oracle can be stated.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The headline result is an existence statement about complex projective varieties. It gives no procedure, interface or data format that a program could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only analytic objects are measures and metric weights on compact complex varieties, used for existence proofs. There is no dynamics or discretisation that a simulation could use. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite étale norm is a gluing construction on abstract sections. It is not a tool with a computable input and output. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main induction assumes the conclusion fails and reaches a contradiction. Several steps use limits and extension of plurisubharmonic weights, so there is no finite step list, termination bound or complexity to state. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Foliations, saturated sheaves and cones are classical geometric objects. The paper gives no representation or operations on them that a data structure could support. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The fibre integrals and plurisubharmonic envelopes are continuous existence arguments. Their total mass need not even be finite without a hypothesis, so no fixed-point or parallel kernel follows. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The results concern complex geometry with no finite field, hardness assumption or security game. A numerical-class statement gives nothing cryptographic. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The metrics are singular Hermitian metrics on line bundles, with a stated meaning for bounded weights. They are not learned models, and nothing in the paper gives a loss or a generalisation statement. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The slope bounds against movable curve classes are used for a pseudoeffectivity argument. They are not an objective, constraint set or solver. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns nodes, messages or consensus. The statement is about a single smooth complex line bundle and its sections. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The value is theoretical, as a nonvanishing theorem for anticanonical bundles in complex algebraic geometry. It has no direct engineering construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-smooth-semipositive-metric** (hypothesis): The headline theorem needs a smooth Hermitian metric of semipositive curvature on the anticanonical bundle. Mere nefness, or numerical effectivity, does not supply it. Blocked use: ['Concluding a section for a variety whose anticanonical bundle is only nef', 'Applying the theorem to a bundle with only a singular metric']
- **limit-complex-smooth-projective** (hypothesis): The ambient setting is a smooth connected projective variety over the complex numbers. Singular, non-projective or positive-characteristic varieties are not covered. Blocked use: ['Using the result on singular or non-projective complex spaces', 'Using the result over fields of positive characteristic']
- **limit-existential-multiple** (nonconstructive): The multiple m is only shown to exist. The proof is by contradiction and induction on dimension, so neither m nor a section is exhibited, and no first-power section is promised. Blocked use: ['Expecting a section of the anticanonical bundle itself', 'Using the theorem to compute m or to write down a section']
- **limit-finite-adjoint-volume** (hypothesis): The induction requires the adjoint measure of the volume metric to have finite total mass. Semipositive curvature alone does not give this. Blocked use: ['Invoking the finite-volume theorem when the adjoint integral diverges']
- **limit-no-forms-and-bounded-weights** (hypothesis): The finite-volume theorem needs the vanishing of all positive-degree holomorphic forms on Z and a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights on L. The pole order b is not uniform. Blocked use: ['Applying it to varieties carrying holomorphic forms of positive degree', 'Using it with unbounded singular weights on L', 'Assuming a uniform bound on b']
- **limit-torus-invariance** (hypothesis): The invariant statement needs a compact connected torus acting holomorphically with the natural linearization. The residual deck action can have infinite image, so the cover need not be a product. Blocked use: ['Assuming a global product decomposition of a finite cover', 'Using an unnatural linearization for invariance']
- **limit-etale-norm-exponent** (edge_case): The norm returns a section only in the power equal to the degree of the cover, so the final exponent depends on the cover and is not uniform. Blocked use: ['Claiming a uniform exponent independent of the cover']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Every smooth connected projective complex variety with smoothly semipositive anticanonical bundle has a nonzero section of some positive anticanonical power.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-headline](#stmt-thm-headline) | theorem | `thm:headline` | 1 | Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective variety over $\mathbb C$. |
| [stmt-thm-finite-volume](#stmt-thm-finite-volume) | theorem | `thm:finite-volume` | 2 | Let $Z$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety of dimension $d$ with $H^0(Z,\Omega_Z^q)=0$ for every $1\le q\le d$. |
| [stmt-thm-structure](#stmt-thm-structure) | theorem | `thm:structure` | 3 | Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety whose anticanonical bundle has a smooth Hermitian metric of semipositive Chern curvature. |
| [stmt-lem-etale-norm](#stmt-lem-etale-norm) | lemma | `lem:etale-norm` | 4 | Let $\nu:S\to X$ be a finite étale morphism of degree $d>0$ between smooth connected projective complex varieties, and let $A$ be a line bundle on $X$. |
| [stmt-lem-prepared-model](#stmt-lem-prepared-model) | lemma | `lem:prepared-model` | 5 | Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective and $S_0$ an integral complex variety. |
| [stmt-h-slope-foliation](#stmt-h-slope-foliation) | lemma | `h:slope:foliation` | 6 | Under the assumptions above, let $\mathcal F\subset T_Z$ be an algebraically integrable saturated foliation with $0<\operatorname{rank}\mathcal F<\dim Z$. |
| [stmt-thm-finite-volume-slope](#stmt-thm-finite-volume-slope) | theorem | `thm:finite-volume-slope` | 7 | Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective over $\mathbb C$, let $D\ge0$ be an integral divisor, and suppose that $J=-K_Z+D$ has a semipositive singular Hermitian metric $h_J$ with |
| [stmt-h-slope-face](#stmt-h-slope-face) | corollary | `h:slope:face` | 8 | Under the hypotheses of Theorem 7, assume $\dim Z>0$ and let $L$ be a pseudoeffective line bundle for which $L+D$ is not big. |
| [stmt-prop-transfer](#stmt-prop-transfer) | proposition | `prop:transfer` | 9 | Let $X,S,Y$ be smooth connected projective varieties over $\mathbb C$, and let $\bar Y$ be a normal projective variety. |
| [stmt-lem-determinant](#stmt-lem-determinant) | lemma | `lem:determinant` | 10 | Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective of dimension $d>0$, with $H^0(Z,\Omega_Z^q)=0$ for $1\le q\le d$. |
| [stmt-lem-maximal-base](#stmt-lem-maximal-base) | lemma | `lem:maximal-base` | 11 | Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective of positive dimension $d$. |
| [stmt-prop-invariant-transfer](#stmt-prop-invariant-transfer) | proposition | `prop:invariant-transfer` | 12 | Use the varieties, maps, line bundles, and section of Proposition 9, with $D=0$. |
| [stmt-thm-invariant](#stmt-thm-invariant) | theorem | `thm:invariant` | 13 | Let $Z$ be smooth connected projective over $\mathbb C$, with $H^0(Z,\Omega_Z^q)=0$ for every $q>0$. |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For a smooth projective complex variety $X$, the anticanonical line bundle is $-K_X=\det T_X$. A smooth Hermitian metric on this line is semipositive when its Chern curvature is a nonnegative real $(1,1)$-form. Such a metric makes the numerical class nef. The nonvanishing question asks whether $H^0(X,-mK_X)\ne0$ for some integer $m>0$, or equivalently whether some positive multiple of $-K_X$ is linearly equivalent to an effective divisor.

We prove the following nonvanishing theorem.

<a id="stmt-thm-headline"></a>
<div id="thm:headline" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Anticanonical nonvanishing). *Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective variety over $\mathbb C$. If $-K_X$ admits a smooth Hermitian metric of semipositive curvature, then $H^0(X,-mK_X)\ne0$ for some integer $m>0$.*

</div>

This is the positive-multiple formulation associated with Yau’s anticanonical nonvanishing question (Yau 1994, Problem 75, p. 395) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Yau1994](#ref-Yau1994)]. The multiple matters even when the anticanonical bundle is not torsion. For a complex Enriques surface $E$, $K_E$ is nontrivial of order two and has no section (Dolgachev 2016, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Dolgachev2016](#ref-Dolgachev2016)]. Write $p_E$ and $p_{\mathbb P^1}$ for the two projections from $E\times\mathbb P^1$. Its anticanonical line is $$p_E^*(-K_E)\otimes p_{\mathbb P^1}^*\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^1}(2).$$ The flat metric on the first factor and Fubini–Study metric on the second make it smoothly semipositive. The product formula for sections gives no first-power section, whereas the square has sections. Restriction to a $\mathbb P^1$ fiber shows that this anticanonical line is not torsion.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Geometry, monodromy, and earlier methods

The structure theory explains both the strength of the metric assumption and the difficulty that remains. Yau’s prescribed-Ricci theorem (Yau 1978) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Yau1978](#ref-Yau1978)] realizes the given smooth semipositive anticanonical curvature as the Ricci form of a Kähler metric. The structure results of Demailly–Peternell–Schneider (Demailly, Peternell, and Schneider 1996, Structure Theorem, p. 218) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[DPS1996](#ref-DPS1996)] and Campana–Demailly–Peternell (Campana, Demailly, and Peternell 2015, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[CDP2015](#ref-CDP2015)] describe the universal cover by its flat, compact Ricci-flat, and remaining compact factors. The remaining factor is projective and has no holomorphic forms of positive degree. After passage to a finite-index deck subgroup, the actions that remain on this factor lie in a compact torus.

This last action cannot be ignored. A section on the compact factor descends only if it is compatible with the deck transformations. The flat and Ricci-flat factors supply canonical frames; on the remaining factor we construct an anticanonical section fixed by the torus, for the natural action on that factor’s anticanonical bundle. Its product with those frames descends to a finite étale cover, and its finite étale norm then gives a section on $X$. This reasoning uses the action on the universal cover, not a global product decomposition of a finite cover.

In the broader nef setting, Lazić–Matsumura–Peternell–Tsakanikas–Xie proved numerical effectivity for projective log canonical threefold pairs with rational boundary and nef log-anticanonical divisor, under $\mathbb Q$-factoriality or rational singularities (Lazić et al. 2023, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[LMPTX2023](#ref-LMPTX2023)]. Numerical effectivity means that the divisor class is numerically equivalent to an effective rational divisor; the existence of a section requires rational *linear* effectivity. For projective klt $\mathbb Q$-pairs with nef log-anticanonical divisor, Müller proved actual nonvanishing in dimension three, and in arbitrary dimension when the log-anticanonical divisor of a general fiber of the maximal rationally connected quotient is semiample (Müller 2025, Theorem A and Corollary B) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Muller2025](#ref-Muller2025)]. His equivariant theorem constructs naturally invariant plurisections of a semiample log-anticanonical divisor for boundary-preserving actions of commutative linear algebraic groups on projective sub-log-canonical $\mathbb Q$-pairs (Müller 2025, Theorem C) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Muller2025](#ref-Muller2025)]. It is a direct methodological predecessor for addressing residual monodromy. Here the finite-volume argument constructs the needed naturally invariant section from smooth semipositivity, without assuming that fiber semiampleness.

In dimension two, Chen–Filip–Sun–Tosatti–Zhang have classified compact Kähler surfaces with Hermitian-semipositive anticanonical bundle (Chen et al. 2026, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[CFSTZ2026](#ref-CFSTZ2026)]. Their classification gives complementary low-dimensional geometry; the present argument develops a section-producing mechanism in arbitrary dimension.[^1]

The ordinary part of the proof builds on two further developments. Generic positivity and foliation methods control cotangent tensors: the relevant inputs come from movable-cone duality (Boucksom et al. 2013) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BDPP2013](#ref-BDPP2013)], movable slope theory (Greb, Kebekus, and Peternell 2016) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[GKP2016](#ref-GKP2016)], and Campana–Păun’s positive-slope algebraicity criterion (Campana and Păun 2019, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[CampanaPaun2019](#ref-CampanaPaun2019)]. The slope argument follows the foliation strategy used by Ou (Ou 2023, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Ou2023](#ref-Ou2023)] and by Lazić–Matsumura–Peternell–Tsakanikas–Xie (Lazić et al. 2023, Theorem 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[LMPTX2023](#ref-LMPTX2023)]. Here the relative Bergman metric supplies ramification-corrected positivity when an effective boundary and a finite adjoint measure replace nef anticanonical data. Hard Lefschetz with multiplier ideals (Demailly, Peternell, and Schneider 2001, Theorem 2.1.1) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[DPS2001](#ref-DPS2001)] then produces twisted forms, whose determinants are the effective divisors used to select a smaller base.

The transfer in Section <a href="#sec:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transfer">5</a> uses the relative Bergman metric of Berndtsson–Păun (Berndtsson and Păun 2008, Theorem 0.1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[BerndtssonPaun2008](#ref-BerndtssonPaun2008)]. The ordinary proof uses its singular-coefficient form. The equivariant proof instead uses Berndtsson’s smooth direct-image positivity (Berndtsson 2009, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Berndtsson2009](#ref-Berndtsson2009)]: averaging is an orthogonal holomorphic projection onto the invariant line. These are distinct applications of positivity, and we state their hypotheses separately.

<a id="sec:overview"></a>
#### A finite-volume theorem and the proof strategy

The inductive statement involves two line bundles. We use additive notation: $J+L$ means $J\otimes L$, and $mL$ means $L^{\otimes m}$. For an effective integral divisor $D$, write $s_D$ for the canonical section of $\mathcal O_Z(D)$. If $J=-K_Z+D$, this section is a $J$-valued holomorphic top form. A metric $h_J$ therefore pairs $s_D$ with its conjugate to give a measure, denoted $|s_D|_{h_J}^2$.

A singular metric has local squared frame norm $e^{-\phi}$. It is semipositive when $\phi$ is plurisubharmonic; locally bounded weights means bounds on both sides.

<a id="stmt-thm-finite-volume"></a>
<div id="thm:finite-volume" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Finite volume and sections with prescribed poles). *Let $Z$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety of dimension $d$ with $H^0(Z,\Omega_Z^q)=0$ for every $1\le q\le d$. Let $D\ge0$ be an integral divisor. Suppose that $J=-K_Z+D$ has a semipositive singular Hermitian metric $h_J$ satisfying $$\int_Z |s_D|_{h_J}^2<\infty.$$ If a line bundle $L$ on $Z$ has a semipositive singular Hermitian metric with locally bounded weights, then there exist integers $a>0$ and $b\ge0$ such that $$H^0(Z,aL+bD)\ne0.$$*

</div>

This generality is needed because a fibration need not transfer the line whose section is sought to the anticanonical bundle of its base. Its fiber integrals instead produce a new target line on the base and a separate corrected anticanonical line carrying the volume metric. The volume metric $h_J$ may be unbounded, provided the indicated measure is integrable. The metric on $L$ has the separate boundedness assumption. The conclusion says that a positive power of $L$ has a rational section with poles only on $D$; the pole order $b$ is not uniform. Rationally connected varieties satisfy the no-forms hypothesis, but the theorem also applies more generally. For example, an Enriques surface has no positive-degree holomorphic forms and a torsion canonical bundle, although it is not rationally connected.

Theorem <a href="#thm:finite-volume" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:finite-volume">2</a> is proved by induction on dimension. We first describe how its components fit together. Assume that its conclusion fails. Then $L+D$ is pseudoeffective but not big. A supporting movable curve class $\alpha\ne0$ satisfies $L\cdot\alpha=D\cdot\alpha=0$. Finite adjoint volume bounds the slopes of cotangent tensors against $\alpha$; in particular their determinant lines have nonpositive degree. This is the content of Section <a href="#sec:slope" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:slope">[sec:slope]</a>.

The absence of holomorphic forms gives $\chi(\mathcal O_Z)=1$. Riemann–Roch and hard Lefschetz then produce sections $$s_i\in H^0(Z,M+m_iL),\qquad m_i\longrightarrow\infty,$$ where $M$ is one fixed cotangent determinant with $M\cdot\alpha\le0$. Consider all nonempty complete systems $|uL+vM+wD|$ with nonnegative integral coefficients. None can define a generically finite map: that would make its divisor big, contradicting its nonpositive pairing with $\alpha$. Choose a system with maximal image dimension and take the relative algebraic closure of its image field in $\mathbb C(Z)$. This gives a rational fibration onto a strictly smaller smooth projective base $S$. Products of sections force every ratio in the indicated systems to be a function on $S$.

This control of section ratios has two consequences. First, ratios among the $s_i$ give a rational section $\rho$ of some $kL$, $k>0$, with no poles on divisors dominating $S$. Second, on a smooth resolution $Y$ with maps $\pi:Y\to Z$ and $f:Y\to S$, put $K_{Y/S}=K_Y-f^*K_S$. The relative adjoint spaces for $K_{Y/S}+\pi^*(J+jkL)$ have dimension one for every $j\ge0$. They are generated over the base field by the pulled-back form $s_D$ with its birational Jacobian, divided by a base canonical frame and multiplied by $\rho^j$. We prove transfer first under these explicit hypotheses; Section <a href="#sec:induction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:induction">6</a> then constructs its inputs and closes the induction, including the extension and common denominator arguments.

Section <a href="#sec:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transfer">5</a> explains why these algebraic facts are the right ones. On a normal equidimensional model, minima of vertical divisor orders turn $\rho$ into a regular section $e$ after subtracting a base line $A$. On a smooth resolution, integrate its powers against the pulled-back adjoint measure. The zeroth integral gives a metric on $-K_S+D_S$ with finite adjoint volume. Normalized high moments give a locally bounded semipositive metric on $A$. Normalization leaves $e$ nonzero on some component above every base divisor. When integrating $e^j$, the boundary estimate therefore has a fixed order, rather than one growing with $j$; dividing its logarithm by $j$ removes that boundary contribution. The correction $D_S$ is supported on base primes for which every component upstairs is exceptional over $Z$ or lies above $D$. Thus the induction hypothesis on $S$ yields a section that lifts with no forbidden pole on $Z$.

To obtain the torus-invariant section needed by the geometric reduction, Section <a href="#sec:torus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:torus">7</a> first proves the smooth invariant transfer statement, Proposition <a href="#prop:invariant-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:invariant-transfer">12</a>, and then applies it to a rational orbit space. Fundamental vector fields give the relative section, and a dense orbit gives invariant adjoint rank one in every degree. Smooth direct-image positivity applies to the invariant line because compact averaging is an orthogonal holomorphic projection. The ordinary theorem on the quotient base then produces a naturally invariant anticanonical section. The cover and norm results of Section <a href="#sec:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:structure">[sec:structure]</a> finish the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:headline" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:headline">1</a>.

The proofs below establish the model preparation, slope bound, and rank and transfer arguments using the cited geometric and analytic results. Section <a href="#sec:models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:models">3</a> introduces the two birational models and the metric conventions used throughout.

<a id="sec:cover"></a>
### Finite covers and residual monodromy

<span id="sec:structure" label="sec:structure"></span>

A smooth semipositive anticanonical metric determines much of the geometry of the universal cover. The remaining issue for sections is the action of the deck group. We record the geometric reduction with that action retained, so that an invariant section on the final compact factor can later be descended to a finite cover of the original variety. The geometric inputs are the Ricci-semipositive structure theorem of Demailly–Peternell–Schneider (Demailly, Peternell, and Schneider 1996, Structure Theorem and §3) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[DPS1996](#ref-DPS1996)] and the rational-connectedness refinement of Campana–Demailly–Peternell (Campana, Demailly, and Peternell 2015, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[CDP2015](#ref-CDP2015)].

<a id="stmt-thm-structure"></a>
<div id="thm:structure" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3** (Finite cover with compact torus monodromy). *Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety whose anticanonical bundle has a smooth Hermitian metric of semipositive Chern curvature. There are a connected finite étale cover $\nu:S\to X$, an integer $a\geq0$, and smooth connected projective varieties $F$ and $Z$ with the following properties.*

<a id="eq:cover-noforms"></a>
1.  *For a suitable Kähler metric on $X$, the common universal cover of $X$ and $S$ has a holomorphic isometric decomposition <a id="eq:cover-product"></a>$$\label{eq:cover-product}
                 \widetilde X=\mathbb C^a\times F\times Z.$$ The compact factors are simply connected. The induced metric on $F$ is Ricci-flat, and $K_F$ has a nowhere-zero parallel holomorphic section. The anticanonical bundle of $Z$ is smoothly semipositive, and $$\label{eq:cover-noforms}
     H^0(Z,\Omega_Z^p)=0\quad(p>0),\qquad
     H^q(Z,\mathcal O_Z)=0\quad(q>0).$$ The variety $Z$ is rationally connected when it has positive dimension.*

<a id="eq:cover-action"></a>
2.  *There are a full lattice $\Lambda\subset\mathbb C^a$ and a homomorphism $\rho:\Lambda\to\operatorname{Aut}(Z)$ for which $$\label{eq:cover-action}
     S=(\mathbb C^a\times F\times Z)/\Lambda,\qquad
     \lambda\cdot(z,f,v)=(z+\lambda,f,\rho(\lambda)v).$$ The closure $T$ of $\rho(\Lambda)$ in the holomorphic isometry group of $Z$ is a compact connected real torus. The trivial torus is allowed.*

<a id="eq:cover-invariants"></a>
3.  *The action of $T$ extends to an algebraic action of a complex algebraic torus $G$ on $Z$, with $T$ Zariski dense in $G$. On $K_Z^{-1}=\det T_Z$ the action is the natural one induced by the differential. For every $m\geq0$, its representation on sections satisfies $$\label{eq:cover-invariants}
           H^0(Z,K_Z^{-m})^T=H^0(Z,K_Z^{-m})^G.$$*

<a id="eq:cover-canonical"></a>
4.  *The canonical bundles of $\mathbb C^a$ and $F$ have nowhere-zero holomorphic frames $\eta_0$ and $\eta_F$ invariant under $\Lambda$. For every integer $m\geq0$, the product projections $\operatorname{pr}_0$, $\operatorname{pr}_F$, and $\operatorname{pr}_Z$ give the natural, $\Lambda$-equivariant identification $$\label{eq:cover-canonical}
     K_{\widetilde X}^{-m}
     =\operatorname{pr}_0^*K_{\mathbb C^a}^{-m}
       \otimes\operatorname{pr}_F^*K_F^{-m}
       \otimes\operatorname{pr}_Z^*K_Z^{-m},$$ in which the first two factors have the invariant frame $\eta_0^{-m}\otimes\eta_F^{-m}$.*

*Any of the factors may be a point. A point contributes the canonical frame $1$; if $a=0$, the lattice is the zero group.*

</div>

The quotient in <a href="#eq:cover-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-action">[eq:cover-action]</a> describes a locally trivial bundle over $\mathbb C^a/\Lambda$. Its monodromy $\rho$ can have infinite image, so the finite cover $S$ need not be a product. This is the reason for retaining the natural torus action on the anticanonical bundle.

<details id="proof-stmt-thm-structure"><summary>Proof of stmt-thm-structure</summary>

*Proof.* We first obtain the product and its cohomological properties. We then choose a finite-index deck subgroup and identify its compact action with an algebraic torus action.

*The metric splitting and the compact factors.* The curvature form of the given metric on $K_X^{-1}$ is a smooth semipositive representative of its first Chern class, with the usual normalizing factor. Yau’s prescribed-Ricci theorem therefore gives a Kähler metric on $X$ whose Ricci form is that curvature form (Yau 1978) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Yau1978](#ref-Yau1978)]; this application is also stated explicitly in (Demailly, Peternell, and Schneider 1996, 218) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[DPS1996](#ref-DPS1996)]. In particular its Ricci tensor is nonnegative. The structure theorem of (Demailly, Peternell, and Schneider 1996) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[DPS1996](#ref-DPS1996)] gives a holomorphic isometric decomposition $$\widetilde X=\mathbb C^a\times\prod_i M_i,$$ where each $M_i$ is compact and simply connected and the nonflat factors are irreducible in the real de Rham decomposition. The compactness of these factors is the Cheeger–Gromoll input in the structure theorem; the holomorphic decomposition comes from the parallel complex structure. Group the Ricci-flat factors into $F$ and the remaining factors into $Z$.

Here is the Bochner argument for the part of this decomposition used below. On a compact Kähler manifold with nonnegative Ricci tensor, a holomorphic $p$-form $u$ is parallel. Moreover, if $\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_n\geq0$ are the Ricci eigenvalues in a unitary frame and $u=\sum_{|J|=p}u_J\,dz_J$, the integrated Bochner formula forces the pointwise vanishing of $$\sum_{|J|=p}\left(\sum_{j\in J}\lambda_j\right)|u_J|^2.$$ These are the Bochner consequences used in (Demailly, Peternell, and Schneider 1996, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[DPS1996](#ref-DPS1996)]; equivalently see the formula in (Campana, Demailly, and Peternell 2015, (1.6)) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[CDP2015](#ref-CDP2015)]. In particular, contraction of $u$ with a positive Ricci eigendirection is zero. If $u\ne0$ on an irreducible factor, its contraction kernel $$\{v\in T^{1,0}M_i:\iota_vu=0\}$$ is a proper parallel complex subbundle. At any point where the Ricci tensor is nonzero, that kernel is also nonzero. Its underlying real subbundle contradicts irreducibility. Thus every non-Ricci-flat factor has no nonzero holomorphic form of positive degree. The decomposition of exterior forms on a product and Kähler Hodge symmetry give <a href="#eq:cover-noforms" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-noforms">[eq:cover-noforms]</a>. Rational connectedness of these factors, including the irreducible compact Hermitian-symmetric case, is the additional conclusion of (Campana, Demailly, and Peternell 2015, Theorem 1.4(a) and §4) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[CDP2015](#ref-CDP2015)]; it is not inferred merely from the vanishing of forms.

The canonical Chern connection of the Ricci-flat factor $F$ is flat. Since $F$ is simply connected, parallel transport gives a nowhere-zero parallel holomorphic frame $\eta_F$ of $K_F$. The metric induced on $K_Z^{-1}$ has semipositive curvature, since the Ricci tensor of the product restricts to that of $Z$.

Projectivity of each compact factor follows directly from that of $X$. Fix points in the other factors and compose its slice in $\widetilde X$ with the covering map to $X$. This holomorphic map has injective differential. Pulling back a positive Hermitian metric on an ample line bundle of $X$ therefore gives a positive line bundle on the compact factor. Kodaira’s embedding theorem makes that factor projective (Kodaira 1954) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Kodaira1954](#ref-Kodaira1954)]. The argument requires the slice map to be immersive, not injective.

*Choosing the deck subgroup.* Let $\Gamma=\pi_1(X)$ act on $\widetilde X$ by deck transformations. These are holomorphic isometries. Uniqueness of the de Rham decomposition implies that, after a finite-index passage removing permutations of its factors, every deck transformation is a product map. Put $M=F\times Z$. Projection of this deck subgroup to the Euclidean isometry group is discrete and has finite kernel. Indeed, for a fixed bound on the displacement of $0\in\mathbb C^a$, a sufficiently large closed Euclidean ball $B$ satisfies $$\gamma(B\times M)\cap(B\times M)\ne\varnothing$$ for every deck transformation within that bound. Since $B\times M$ is compact, proper discontinuity allows only finitely many such transformations. This proves both assertions. The Euclidean image is cocompact, by compactness of the quotient of the full product. Bieberbach’s theorem supplies a finite-index translation lattice in this image, as in (Demailly, Peternell, and Schneider 1996, sec. 3(ii)) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[DPS1996](#ref-DPS1996)].

For completeness, the finite kernel does not obstruct choosing an actual lattice subgroup of the deck group. Above the translation lattice we have an extension $$1\longrightarrow N\longrightarrow\Gamma_1
        \longrightarrow\Lambda_1\longrightarrow 0$$ with $N$ finite. Pass to the centralizer of $N$ in $\Gamma_1$; this has finite index because $\operatorname{Aut}(N)$ is finite. Its kernel $N'$ is central, and its image $\Lambda'$ is a sublattice of finite index in $\Lambda_1$. Choose lifts $g_1,\ldots,g_{2a}$ of a lattice basis $\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_{2a}$ of $\Lambda'$. Their commutators belong to $N'$. If $e>0$ is divisible by the orders of all elements of $N'$, then $$[g_i^e,g_j^e]=[g_i,g_j]^{e^2}=1.$$ The commuting elements $g_i^e$ generate a free abelian subgroup: a relation among them projects to a relation among the independent vectors $e\lambda_i$. Its image is $e\Lambda'$, so this subgroup has finite index in the original deck group. In case $a=0$, the deck group is finite and we instead take its trivial subgroup.

We next eliminate the action on $F$. The holomorphic isometry group of a compact simply connected Ricci-flat manifold is finite. To see this, its Lie algebra consists of Killing fields. The integrated Bochner identity makes each such field parallel. Its dual one-form is closed and, by simple connectedness, exact. An exact parallel one-form on a compact manifold is zero: a primitive attains a maximum, where its differential vanishes. Thus the compact isometry group has zero-dimensional Lie algebra and is finite. Passing to the kernel of the action on $F$ preserves a finite-index lattice subgroup.

The remaining action on $Z$ has commuting image in its compact holomorphic isometry group. Its closure is a compact abelian Lie group. Its identity component is a torus and its component group is finite. Pass once more to the preimage of that identity component. The new image is dense in the identity component, since the latter is open in the old closure. Denote this connected closure by $T$, the deck lattice by $\Lambda$, and its action on $Z$ by $\rho$. This proves <a href="#eq:cover-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-action">[eq:cover-action]</a>.

The corresponding quotient $S$ is a connected finite unramified holomorphic cover of $X$. The pulled-back positive line bundle makes $S$ projective, and the covering is a finite étale morphism of projective varieties. We have now accounted for every part of the deck action. In particular $\eta_F$ is invariant because the action on $F$ is trivial, while $\eta_0=dz_1\wedge\cdots\wedge dz_a$ is invariant under translations. The determinant of the product tangent decomposition gives <a href="#eq:cover-canonical" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-canonical">[eq:cover-canonical]</a>, with its natural equivariance.

*The algebraic torus.* It remains to put the compact action on $Z$ in the algebraic category. The point case is immediate, so suppose $\dim Z>0$ and choose a very ample line bundle $A$ on $Z$. By <a href="#eq:cover-noforms" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-noforms">[eq:cover-noforms]</a>, the exponential sequence makes $$c_1:\operatorname{Pic}(Z)\longrightarrow
                      H^2(Z,\mathbb Z)$$ injective. Every element of the connected group $T$ is isotopic to the identity and hence acts trivially on integral cohomology. Consequently $t^*A\simeq A$ for every $t\in T$. The complete linear system of $A$ therefore defines a canonical projective representation $$T\longrightarrow
           \operatorname{PGL}\bigl(H^0(Z,A)^*\bigr)$$ preserving the embedded variety $Z$. Choices of isomorphisms $t^*A\simeq A$ change the associated linear maps only by scalars, so this projective representation is well-defined. It is continuous: choose a projective frame from the nondegenerate irreducible embedded variety; a projective transformation is determined continuously by the images of that frame. Such a frame exists by first choosing a projective basis and then a point outside its finitely many coordinate hyperplanes.

Write $r=\dim H^0(Z,A)$. To diagonalize the compact projective action, take its compact preimage under the finite covering $\operatorname{SL}_r(\mathbb C)\to\operatorname{PGL}_r(\mathbb C)$. The identity component of this preimage is abelian and surjects onto $T$: its Lie algebra maps isomorphically to the abelian Lie algebra of $T$, and its image is an open and closed subgroup of $T$. Averaging a Hermitian form makes this connected preimage unitary; its commuting matrices can then be diagonalized simultaneously. Thus, after conjugation, $T$ lies in a diagonal algebraic torus in $\operatorname{PGL}_r(\mathbb C)$.

Let $G$ be its Zariski closure there. It is connected, since a connected $T$ cannot meet distinct components of an algebraic group without being disconnected. A connected algebraic subgroup of a diagonal torus is an algebraic torus. The projective stabilizer of $Z$ is Zariski closed, so $G$ preserves $Z$ and acts algebraically. Differentiation gives the natural algebraic linearization on $K_Z^{-1}$. The induced finite-dimensional representation on $H^0(Z,K_Z^{-m})$ is algebraic. The stabilizer of any section is Zariski closed, and Zariski density of $T$ proves <a href="#eq:cover-invariants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-invariants">[eq:cover-invariants]</a>. All the constructions respect point factors and the stated canonical-frame conventions. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### The norm of a section
